<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We establish the quasi-Riemann hypothesis by proving that every Dirichlet <i>L</i>-function, including Riemann's zeta function, has no zeros in the half-plane $`\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\nolimits s\gt 11/12`$. More generally, we prove the same zero-free half-plane for every finite-order Hecke <i>L</i>-function over $`K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})`$. In particular, this rules out the existence of Landau–Siegel zeros.

- **claim-zero-free-half-plane** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every finite-order Hecke $L$-function over $K$ has no zeros in the half-plane $\operatorname{Re}s>11/12$. Thus every Dirichlet $L$-function, including $\zeta(s)$, has no zeros for $\operatorname{Re}s>11/12$.
  - Explanation: Asserts a uniform zero-free half-plane for finite-order Hecke functions over the specified Eisenstein field, and transfers nonvanishing to Dirichlet functions.
- **claim-primes-in-progressions** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-primes-ap)): Let $\pi(x;q,a)$ count the primes $p\le x$ with $p\equiv a\pmod q$. Write $\varphi$ for Euler’s totient function. For $x\ge2$, $$\sup_{\substack{1\le q\le x\\(a,q)=1}} \biggl\|\pi(x;q,a)-\frac1{\varphi(q)} \int_2^x\frac{\,dt}{\log t}\biggr\| \ll x^{11/12}\log x,$$ where the implied constant is absolute and effective.
  - Explanation: Bounds the discrepancy between prime counting in coprime arithmetic progressions and the stated integral main term, uniformly over the stated modulus range.
- **claim-twisted-mobius-mean-square** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-ms)): For every fixed $0<\vartheta\le1/10$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there exists an integer $k=k(\vartheta,\varepsilon)\ge1$ such that, for every compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, all smooth $W$ supported in $I$, and $D\ge2$, $$\label{eq:ms} \sum_{0<\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(u)\le D^{1+\vartheta}}\|A_u(D)\|^2 \ll_{\nu,S,I,\vartheta,\varepsilon} \Bigl(\max_{0\le j\le k}\\|W^{(j)}\\|_\infty\Bigr)^2 D^{2+\vartheta+\varepsilon}.$$
  - Explanation: Controls the mean square of the sextic-twisted Möbius family through a finite derivative norm of the common smooth weight.
- **claim-ray-class-arithmetic-identities** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-arithmetic)): On a fixed ray class group, there are a function $G$ with values in $\{z\in\mathbb C:\|z\|=1\}$ and a symmetric $\{\pm1\}$-valued bicharacter $\mathcal R$. This means that $\mathcal R(a,b)=\mathcal R(b,a)$ and $\mathcal R$ is multiplicative in each argument separately: $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal R(aa',b)&=\mathcal R(a,b)\mathcal R(a',b),\\ \mathcal R(a,bb')&=\mathcal R(a,b)\mathcal R(a,b'), \end{aligned}$$ for all classes $a,a',b,b'$ in the ray class group. These functions have the following properties. Let $a,b,n$ be primary elements prime to $S$, with $(a,b)=1$ and $n$ squarefree. Then $$\begin{aligned} \chi_b(a)&=\mathcal R(a,b)\chi_a(b),& G(ab)&=G(a)G(b)\mathcal R(a,b), \label{eq:recip}\\ \gamma_2(n)^3&=\mu(n)\alpha(n),& \gamma_1(n)\gamma_2(n)&=\mu(n)\alpha(n)G(n), \label{eq:gj}\\ G(n)&=\overline{\chi_n(4)}\gamma_3(n),& \gamma_1(n)\gamma_{-1}(n)&=\chi_n(-1). \label{eq:g} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\begin{aligned} \overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n)\gamma_1(n)&=\mu(n)G(n),\label{eq:convert1}\\ \mu(n)\gamma_{-1}(n) &=\chi_n(-1)G(n)^{-1}\overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n),\label{eq:convert2}\\ a_\xi(ab)&=a_\xi(a)a_\xi(b)\chi_b(a)^4, \label{eq:crt-a}\\ \chi_a(-1)\overline{G(a)}G(b)\mathcal R(a,b)&=G(ba^{-1}). \label{eq:quotient} \end{aligned}$$ In <a href="#eq:crt-a" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:crt-a">[eq:crt-a]</a>, $a,b$ are also squarefree. The identity for $G(ab)$ in <a href="#eq:recip" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:recip">[eq:recip]</a> extends to all classes of the fixed ray class group; the quotient in <a href="#eq:quotient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quotient">[eq:quotient]</a> is taken in that group.
  - Explanation: Supplies the fixed ray class factors, reciprocity identities, and coefficient conversions needed to alternate between Möbius and cubic Gauss-sum coefficients.
- **claim-poisson-with-exclusions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-poisson)): Let $\mathcal H>0$, let $\mathfrak r$ be a nonzero ideal, and let $\Phi(\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(k)/\mathcal H)$ be a smooth radial Schwartz weight on the row lattice. Let $\chi$ be a primitive multiplicative character of $(\mathcal O/\mathfrak m)^\times$, viewed as a function on $\mathcal O$ by reduction modulo $\mathfrak m$ and extension by zero on nonunits. Write $$\gamma(\chi)=\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\mathfrak m)^{-1/2} \sum_{x\bmod\mathfrak m}\chi(x)e(x/\mathfrak m)$$ for its normalized Gauss sum. Since $\chi$ is primitive, its modulus $\mathfrak m$ is determined by $\chi$ and is suppressed in the notation. Then $$\label{eq:poisson} \begin{aligned} &\sum_k\chi(k)\mathbf 1_{(k,\mathfrak r)=1}\Phi(\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(k)/\mathcal{H})\\ &\quad=\frac{\mathcal{H}\gamma(\chi)}{\sqrt{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\mathfrak m)}} \sum_{d\mid\operatorname{rad}\mathfrak r}\frac{\mu(d)\chi(d)}{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(d)} \sum_h\overline{\chi(h)} \widehat\Phi\!\Bigl(\frac{\mathcal{H}\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(h)}{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(d)\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\mathfrak m)}\Bigr). \end{aligned}$$ Here $\widehat\Phi$ is defined by $$\widehat\Phi(\|w\|^2)=\frac2{\sqrt3}\int_{\mathbb C} \Phi(\|z\|^2)e(-zw)\,dx\,dy,\qquad z=x+\mathrm i y.$$ The measure is normalized so that $\mathcal O$ has covolume one. Here $k,h\in\mathcal O$, and $h$ is the Fourier frequency. We have $$\chi\text{ nonprincipal}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \overline{\chi(0)}=0.$$ For the principal primitive character ($\chi=\mathbf1$, $\mathfrak m=1$), the zero-frequency contribution is $$\mathcal H\widehat\Phi(0) \prod_{\mathfrak p\mid \mathfrak r}\Bigl(1-\frac1{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\mathfrak p)}\Bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Gives lattice Poisson summation with an explicit divisor expansion for excluded primes, including the distinction between principal and nonprincipal zero frequencies.
- **claim-remove-column-exclusions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-remove-exclusions)): Let $r_0$ be the product of the primes dividing $\mathfrak r$ outside $S$. For $\mathcal H,X>0$, $F\ge1$, and smooth compactly supported $W$, $$\label{eq:remove-exclusions} \mathcal E_{\mathfrak r}(\mathcal H,X,F;\xi,W) \le\tau_{\mathrm{div}}(r_0) \sum_{d\mid r_0}\mathcal E\!\Bigl(\mathcal H,\frac X{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(d)},F\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(d);\xi,W\Bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Removes column coprimality restrictions by summing over divisors and shifting the column and auxiliary scales while preserving their product.
- **claim-original-to-dual-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-poisson-reduction)): Fix $0<\vartheta\le1/10$ and put $H=D^{1+\vartheta}$. For each fixed $C\ge1$ and all real $B,F\ge1$, consider the ranges $$\label{eq:initial-scales} X=\frac D{BF},\qquad 0<\mathcal{H}\le\frac{CD^2}{HB^2},\qquad XF=\frac DB.$$ Suppose that for every $\varepsilon>0$ there exists an integer $J=J(\vartheta,\varepsilon)\ge1$ such that, for every compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$ and every smooth $W$ supported in $I$, $$\label{eq:auxiliary-target} \mathcal E(\mathcal H,X,F;\xi,W) \ll_{\nu,S,I,C,\vartheta,\varepsilon}\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2D^\varepsilon XF,$$ where $$\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}=\max_{0\le j\le J}\sup_{x\in I}\|W^{(j)}(x)\|.$$ For functions of several variables, the norm uses all partial derivatives of total order at most $J$. Then the original mean-square estimate in Proposition <a href="#thm:ms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ms">3</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Shows that a uniform dual mean-square estimate in the initial scale ranges implies the original Möbius mean-square estimate.
- **claim-canonical-positive-gap-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-canonical)): Fix $\kappa>0$ and $C_0\ge1$. Suppose that $$\label{eq:positive-gap} \mathcal H,X,F\ge1,\qquad \Sigma=XF\le D^{C_0},\qquad \mathcal H\le\Sigma D^{-\kappa}.$$ For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is an integer $J=J(\kappa,C_0,\varepsilon)\ge1$ such that $$\label{eq:canonical-bound} \mathcal E(\mathcal H,X,F;\xi,W) \ll_{I,\nu,S,\kappa,C_0,\varepsilon} \\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2D^\varepsilon\Sigma$$ for every compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$ and smooth $W$ supported in $I$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the dual energy by the target product scale when the row scale has a fixed power gap below that product.
- **claim-completed-mean-square** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-r)): Fix $\varepsilon>0$, $C_0\ge1$, and a character $\xi$ of the fixed ray class group. There is $J=J(\varepsilon,C_0)\ge1$ such that $$\label{eq:R} \sum_{0<\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(k)\ll\mathcal H}\|T(X;k,f)\|^2 \ll_{I,\xi,S,\varepsilon,C_0} D^\varepsilon\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2 \Bigl(\mathcal H+\frac{\mathcal H^2\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(f)}{X}\Bigr)$$ whenever $$1\le\mathcal H,X,\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(f)\le D^{C_0}.$$ Here $f$ is squarefree and primary with $(f,S)=1$, $W$ is smooth and supported in a compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, and $T$ is defined by <a href="#eq:completed-twist" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:completed-twist">[eq:completed-twist]</a>.
  - Explanation: Controls completed theta sums over arbitrary rows, with the auxiliary twist entering the second term of the bound.
- **claim-cube-inversion-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cube-reduction)): Fix $C_0\ge1$ and $\varepsilon>0$. Suppose $1\le\mathcal H,X,F\le D^{C_0}$ and $\mathcal H\le\Sigma=XF$. There is an integer $J=J(\varepsilon,C_0)\ge1$ such that $$\label{eq:cube-reduction} \mathcal E(\mathcal H,X,F;\xi,W) \ll_{I,\nu,S,C_0,\varepsilon}D^\varepsilon \biggl(\Sigma\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2+ \sup_{\substack{b\equiv1\ (3),\ (b,S)=1\\ \mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(b)>H_c,\ L_b>1}} \mathcal E(\mathcal H,L_b,F;\xi,W)\biggr)$$ for every compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$ and $W\in C_c^\infty(I)$. An empty supremum is zero; in particular it is empty when $\mathcal H^2\le X$.
  - Explanation: Separates the energy into a directly controlled contribution and a supremum over shorter column scales from large cube divisors.
- **claim-contracting-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-transfer)): Assume $1\le\mathcal H,L,F,\Sigma\le D^{C_0}$ and $\max\{\mathcal H,LF\}\le\Sigma$, for fixed $C_0\ge1$. For every integer $m\ge0$ and $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:transfer-summary} \mathcal A(W)\ll D^\varepsilon\Sigma\\|W\\|_{C^{4m+12}(I)}^2 \Bigl(1+\sup\frac{\mathcal E(\mathcal H',X',F';\xi',U)}{\Sigma'}\Bigr)$$ where the supremum is over the family <a href="#eq:energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy">[eq:energy]</a>, with $\Sigma'=X'F'$, $\mathcal H',X',F'\ge1$, and $$\label{eq:transfer-scales} \mathcal H'\le\frac{\mathcal HL}{\Sigma F},\qquad \frac{\mathcal H'}{\Sigma'}\le \frac{\mathcal H}{\Sigma},\qquad \Sigma'\le L.$$ For $I=[u,v]$, the tests satisfy $U\in C_c^\infty(I')$, $I'=[u/16,4v]$, and $\\|U\\|_{C^m(I')}\le1$; an empty supremum is zero. The estimate holds for every compact $I\subset(0,\infty)$ and $W\in C_c^\infty(I)$, with implied constant depending on $m,C_0,\varepsilon,I,\nu,S$ and the fixed ray class group.
  - Explanation: Bounds the smoothed energy by child energies whose row scale contracts and whose row-to-target ratio does not increase.
- **claim-theta-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-realization)): With these definitions, $$\label{eq:T-theta} T(X;k,f)=\frac1{3^{5/2}\sqrt X} \sum_{\substack{n,b\in\mathcal O\\n,b\equiv1\ (3)\\n\ {\rm squarefree}\\(nb,S)=1}} c_\theta(nb^3)\phi_k(nb^3)\overline{\alpha(nb^3)} W\!\Bigl(\frac{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(nb^3)}X\Bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:theta-twist-translates} \Theta_k(z,v)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal O/(q)}\widehat\phi_k(h) \overline{\theta(z+\lambda^2h/q,v)}.$$
  - Explanation: Identifies the completed sum with twisted cubic theta coefficients and realizes the twisted theta function as a finite sum of translates.
- **claim-theta-reflection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reflection)): The quantity $T(X;\Psi)$ defined in <a href="#eq:T" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:T">[eq:T]</a> is a sum of $O_{\Psi_0,S}(2^{\|\mathcal P\|})$ terms of the form $$\label{eq:reflection} C\sum_{0\ne\ell\in\lambda^{-4}\mathcal O} \frac{d(\ell)\alpha(\ell)}{\sqrt{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\ell)}}\, \psi(\lambda^4\ell) \prod_{p\in\mathcal A}B_{p,j_p}(\lambda^4\ell) V_*^\sharp\!\Bigl(\frac{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\ell) X}{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(c)^2}\Bigr).$$* *Here $\mathcal A$ and $c$ are as above, and $\|C\|\ll_{\Psi_0,S}1$. The triples $(d,\psi,c_0)$ belong to a fixed finite family depending only on $\Psi_0,S$, where $d\in\{d_0,d_+,d_-\}$, $c_0\in\mathcal O\setminus\{0\}$, and $\psi$ is a unit-modulus additive character on $\mathcal O$.
  - Explanation: Transforms the completed sum into dual sums with cusp coefficients, local character factors, and a transformed smooth weight.
- **claim-reflection-ray-class-uniformity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reflection-uniformity)): For the family $\Psi_{k_0}$ above, the summands in Proposition <a href="#lem:reflection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:reflection">14</a> may be indexed by $(h,\mathcal A_{\rm fix})$, with $h$ in a fixed finite set depending only on $\Psi_0,S$. Zero scalar coefficients are permitted. For each fixed index, the triple $(d,\psi,c_0)$ depends on $k_0$ only through its ray class modulo a fixed ideal supported on $S$ and depending only on $\Psi_0,S$.
  - Explanation: Makes the cusp coefficient sequences and additive twists uniform within fixed ray classes of the varying squarefree row.
- **claim-theta-support-and-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-bounds)): For each $d\in\{d_0,d_+,d_-\}$, the coefficient $d(\ell)$ vanishes unless $\ell$ can be written as $$\label{eq:theta-support} \ell=u\lambda^mnb^3,$$ where $u\in\mathcal O^\times$, $m\in\mathbb Z$ with $m\ge-4$, and $n,b\in\mathcal O$ are primary with $n$ squarefree. $$\label{eq:theta-coefficient-bound} \|d(\ell)\|=\|d(u\lambda^mnb^3)\|\le27\cdot 3^{m/6}\|b\|.$$* *For $A>0$, integers $j\ge0$, and $V_*$ supported in a fixed compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, there is $J=J(A,j)$ such that $$\label{eq:theta-weight-decay} \|(x\partial_x)^jV_*^\sharp(x)\| \ll_{A,j,I}\\|V_*\\|_{C^J(I)}\min(x^{1/4},x^{-A}),\qquad x>0.$$
  - Explanation: Restricts the support and magnitude of cusp coefficients and bounds derivatives of the transformed weight near the origin and at infinity.
- **claim-quadratic-large-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic)): Let $\mathcal H,U\ge1$, and let $\beta(n)$ be complex coefficients on squarefree primary $n$ with $\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(n)\asymp U$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:Q} \sum_{\substack{k\equiv1\ (3),\ (k,S)=1\\\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(k)\le\mathcal H}}^* \Bigl\|\sum_{\substack{n\equiv1\ (3),\ n\ {\rm squarefree}\\ \mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(n)\asymp U}} \beta(n)\chi_k(n)^3\Bigr\|^2 \ll_{S,\varepsilon}(\mathcal HU)^\varepsilon(\mathcal H+U) \sum_n\|\beta(n)\|^2.$$ The star restricts $k$ to squarefree primary elements.
  - Explanation: Bounds the mean square of quadratic character sums by the sum of the row and column scales times the coefficient energy, allowing a small power loss.
- **claim-squarefree-completed-rows** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-squarefree-completed)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ and $C_0\ge1$ there is $J=J(\varepsilon,C_0)\ge1$ such that $$\label{eq:squarefree-completed} \sum_{\substack{0<N(s)\le\mathcal H\\s\ {\rm squarefree}}} \|T(X;u_0s,g)\|^2 \ll_{I,\xi,S,\varepsilon,C_0}D^\varepsilon\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2 \Bigl(\mathcal H+\frac{\mathcal H^2N(g)}X\Bigr)$$ for $1\le\mathcal H,X,N(g)\le D^{C_0}$, $u_0\in\mathcal O^\times$, every compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, and $W\in C_c^\infty(I)$. The sum uses the chosen generators of squarefree ideals, including those meeting $S$.
  - Explanation: Bounds completed sums over squarefree ideal rows, permitting rows meeting the excluded prime set and a general auxiliary twist.
- **claim-first-transfer-control** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-first-transfer)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transfer">12</a>, fix an integer $j\ge0$ and $\varepsilon_0>0$. If $M\ge0$ satisfies $\mathcal Q_{\xi_1}(U)\le M\\|U\\|_{C^j(I_*)}^2$ for every $U\in C_c^\infty(I_*)$ and every $\xi_1$, then $$\mathcal A(W)\ll D^{\varepsilon_0}(\Sigma+M) \\|W\\|_{C^{2j+4}(I)}^2.$$
  - Explanation: Converts a common-test-function bound for the auxiliary nonnegative form into a bound for the smoothed original energy.
- **claim-second-poisson-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arithmetic-poisson)): With the notation and index ranges above, $$\label{eq:arithmetic-poisson-identity} \mathcal M=Z+ \sum_{g,e,w,h,\xi'}\frac{Y\mu(e)\mu(w)c_{\xi'}N(g)}{N(e)\ell} \sum_{(n_1n_2,S)=1}^*a^\sharp(n_1)\overline{a^\sharp(n_2)} \mathscr K\!\Bigl(\frac{N(n_1)}{X'},\frac{N(n_2)}{X'}\Bigr).$$ The zero-frequency term satisfies $$\|Z\|\ll_{I_*,\Phi}Y\ell\\|U\\|_\infty^2.$$
  - Explanation: Expresses the auxiliary Möbius mean square as a zero-frequency contribution plus dual cubic Gauss-sum terms with explicit exclusions and weights.
- **claim-second-transfer-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-second-transfer)): Return to $I=[u,v]$ and $I_*=[u/2,2v]$. For an integer $m\ge0$, let $S_m$ be the supremum in <a href="#eq:transfer-summary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transfer-summary">[eq:transfer-summary]</a>. Then, for every $\varepsilon_0>0$, $$\mathcal Q_{\xi_1}(U)\ll D^{\varepsilon_0}\Sigma(1+S_m) \\|U\\|_{C^{2m+4}(I_*)}^2 \qquad(U\in C_c^\infty(I_*)).$$ The test functions in $S_m$ are supported in $I'=[u/16,4v]$ and have $C^m(I')$ norm at most one; an empty supremum is zero.
  - Explanation: Controls the auxiliary form by normalized child energies after regrouping the second Poisson output and separating its smooth kernels.
- **claim-smooth-mellin-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-smooth)): Fix a box $\mathcal I=I_1\times\cdots\times I_d$, where each $I_j$ is a compact interval in $(0,\infty)$. Every $\mathcal K\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty)^d)$ supported in $\mathcal I$ has a representation $$\label{eq:smooth-separation} \mathcal K(\mathbf x)=\int_{\mathbb R^d}b(\mathbf t) \prod_{j=1}^d x_j^{\mathrm i t_j}\,d\mathbf t, \qquad x_j>0.$$ For $J\ge0$ and every even integer $q>J+d$, the coefficient satisfies $$\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|b(\mathbf t)\|(1+\|\mathbf t\|)^J\,d\mathbf t \ll_{\mathcal I,J,q,d}\\|\mathcal K\\|_{C^q(\mathcal I)}.$$
  - Explanation: Separates a smooth compactly supported kernel into multiplicative phases and controls the coefficient moments using finitely many derivatives.
- **claim-row-dependent-kernel-bound** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-smooth-mean-square)): Consider two families of finite sums $$S_{j,r}(U)=\sum_n a_{j,r}(n)U(x_{j,r,n}),\qquad j=1,2,\quad x_{j,r,n}>0,$$ with a finite set of rows $r$ and nonnegative weights $w_r$. Suppose that, for every $U\in C_c^\infty(I_*)$, $$\sum_r w_r\|S_{j,r}(U)\|^2\le M_j\\|U\\|_{C^m(I_*)}^2, \qquad j=1,2.$$ Then for any $\|c_r\|\le w_r$ and smooth kernels $\mathcal K_r$ supported in $I^2$, $$\label{eq:coupled-mean-square} \Bigl\|\sum_r c_r\sum_{n_1,n_2} a_{1,r}(n_1)\overline{a_{2,r}(n_2)} \mathcal K_r(x_{1,r,n_1},x_{2,r,n_2})\Bigr\| \ll_{I,I_*,m}\sqrt{M_1M_2}\, \sup_r\\|\mathcal K_r\\|_{C^{2m+4}(I^2)}.$$
  - Explanation: Extends common-weight mean-square estimates to bilinear sums with row-dependent smooth kernels, paying a finite derivative cost.
- **claim-weighted-recombination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-recombine)): Let $r,\iota$ range over finite sets, let $d\ge1$, and let $w_r\ge0$. Suppose the measurable coefficients $c_{\iota,r}$ satisfy $$\|c_{\iota,r}(\mathbf t)\|\le b_\iota(\mathbf t),\qquad M:=\sum_\iota\int_{\mathbb R^d}b_\iota(\mathbf t)\,d\mathbf t<\infty,$$ where $b_\iota:\mathbb R^d\to[0,\infty)$. For measurable $F_{\iota,\mathbf t}(r)$, whenever the right-hand side is finite, one has $$\label{eq:integral-mean-square} \sum_r w_r\Bigl\|\sum_\iota\int c_{\iota,r}(\mathbf t) F_{\iota,\mathbf t}(r)\,d\mathbf t\Bigr\|^2 \le M\sum_\iota\int b_\iota(\mathbf t) \sum_r w_r\|F_{\iota,\mathbf t}(r)\|^2\,d\mathbf t.$$ The same assertion holds for finite sums with the integrals omitted.
  - Explanation: Recombines separated sums or integrals under a common integrable majorant by weighted Cauchy–Schwarz.

        "exact_quote": "On a fixed ray class group, there are a function $G$ with values in $\\{z\\in\\mathbb C:|z|=1\\}$ and a symmetric $\\{\\pm1\\}$-valued bicharacter $\\mathcal R$. This means that $\\mathcal R(a,b)=\\mathcal R(b,a)$ and $\\mathcal R$ is multiplicative in each argument separately: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal R(aa',b)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a',b),\\\\\n \\mathcal R(a,bb')&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a,b'),\n \\end{aligned}$$ for all classes $a,a',b,b'$ in the ray class group. These functions have the following properties. Let $a,b,n$ be primary elements prime to $S$, with $(a,b)=1$ and $n$ squarefree. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi_b(a)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\chi_a(b),&\n G(ab)&=G(a)G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b),                                      \\label{eq:recip}\\\\\n \\gamma_2(n)^3&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_2(n)&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n)G(n),                 \\label{eq:gj}\\\\\n G(n)&=\\overline{\\chi_n(4)}\\gamma_3(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)&=\\chi_n(-1).                     \\label{eq:g}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n)\\gamma_1(n)&=\\mu(n)G(n),\\label{eq:convert1}\\\\\n \\mu(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)\n &=\\chi_n(-1)G(n)^{-1}\\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n),\\label{eq:convert2}\\\\\n a_\\xi(ab)&=a_\\xi(a)a_\\xi(b)\\chi_b(a)^4,                     \\label{eq:crt-a}\\\\\n \\chi_a(-1)\\overline{G(a)}G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b)&=G(ba^{-1}).            \\label{eq:quotient}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In <a href=\"#eq:crt-a\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:crt-a\">[eq:crt-a]</a>, $a,b$ are also squarefree. The identity for $G(ab)$ in <a href=\"#eq:recip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:recip\">[eq:recip]</a> extends to all classes of the fixed ray class group; the quotient in <a href=\"#eq:quotient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient\">[eq:quotient]</a> is taken in that group.",
        "source_id": "PM-003.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arithmetic",
          "exact_quote": "On a fixed ray class group, there are a function $G$ with values in $\\{z\\in\\mathbb C:|z|=1\\}$ and a symmetric $\\{\\pm1\\}$-valued bicharacter $\\mathcal R$. This means that $\\mathcal R(a,b)=\\mathcal R(b,a)$ and $\\mathcal R$ is multiplicative in each argument separately: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal R(aa',b)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a',b),\\\\\n \\mathcal R(a,bb')&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a,b'),\n \\end{aligned}$$ for all classes $a,a',b,b'$ in the ray class group. These functions have the following properties. Let $a,b,n$ be primary elements prime to $S$, with $(a,b)=1$ and $n$ squarefree. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi_b(a)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\chi_a(b),&\n G(ab)&=G(a)G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b),                                      \\label{eq:recip}\\\\\n \\gamma_2(n)^3&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_2(n)&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n)G(n),                 \\label{eq:gj}\\\\\n G(n)&=\\overline{\\chi_n(4)}\\gamma_3(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)&=\\chi_n(-1).                     \\label{eq:g}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n)\\gamma_1(n)&=\\mu(n)G(n),\\label{eq:convert1}\\\\\n \\mu(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)\n &=\\chi_n(-1)G(n)^{-1}\\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n),\\label{eq:convert2}\\\\\n a_\\xi(ab)&=a_\\xi(a)a_\\xi(b)\\chi_b(a)^4,                     \\label{eq:crt-a}\\\\\n \\chi_a(-1)\\overline{G(a)}G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b)&=G(ba^{-1}).            \\label{eq:quotient}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In <a href=\"#eq:crt-a\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:crt-a\">[eq:crt-a]</a>, $a,b$ are also squarefree. The identity for $G(ab)$ in <a href=\"#eq:recip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:recip\">[eq:recip]</a> extends to all classes of the fixed ray class group; the quotient in <a href=\"#eq:quotient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient\">[eq:quotient]</a> is taken in that group.",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On a fixed ray class group, there are a function $G$ with values in $\\{z\\in\\mathbb C:|z|=1\\}$ and a symmetric $\\{\\pm1\\}$-valued bicharacter $\\mathcal R$. This means that $\\mathcal R(a,b)=\\mathcal R(b,a)$ and $\\mathcal R$ is multiplicative in each argument separately: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal R(aa',b)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a',b),\\\\\n \\mathcal R(a,bb')&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a,b'),\n \\end{aligned}$$ for all classes $a,a',b,b'$ in the ray class group. These functions have the following properties. Let $a,b,n$ be primary elements prime to $S$, with $(a,b)=1$ and $n$ squarefree. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi_b(a)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\chi_a(b),&\n G(ab)&=G(a)G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b),                                      \\label{eq:recip}\\\\\n \\gamma_2(n)^3&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_2(n)&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n)G(n),                 \\label{eq:gj}\\\\\n G(n)&=\\overline{\\chi_n(4)}\\gamma_3(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)&=\\chi_n(-1).                     \\label{eq:g}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n)\\gamma_1(n)&=\\mu(n)G(n),\\label{eq:convert1}\\\\\n \\mu(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)\n &=\\chi_n(-1)G(n)^{-1}\\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n),\\label{eq:convert2}\\\\\n a_\\xi(ab)&=a_\\xi(a)a_\\xi(b)\\chi_b(a)^4,                     \\label{eq:crt-a}\\\\\n \\chi_a(-1)\\overline{G(a)}G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b)&=G(ba^{-1}).            \\label{eq:quotient}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In <a href=\"#eq:crt-a\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:crt-a\">[eq:crt-a]</a>, $a,b$ are also squarefree. The identity for $G(ab)$ in <a href=\"#eq:recip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:recip\">[eq:recip]</a> extends to all classes of the fixed ray class group; the quotient in <a href=\"#eq:quotient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient\">[eq:quotient]</a> is taken in that group.",
      "explanation": "Supplies the fixed ray class factors, reciprocity identities, and coefficient conversions needed to alternate between Möbius and cubic Gauss-sum coefficients.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-poisson",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H>0$, let $\\mathfrak r$ be a nonzero ideal, and let $\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal H)$ be a smooth radial Schwartz weight on the row lattice. Let $\\chi$ be a primitive multiplicative character of $(\\mathcal O/\\mathfrak m)^\\times$, viewed as a function on $\\mathcal O$ by reduction modulo $\\mathfrak m$ and extension by zero on nonunits. Write $$\\gamma(\\chi)=\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)^{-1/2}\n\\sum_{x\\bmod\\mathfrak m}\\chi(x)e(x/\\mathfrak m)$$ for its normalized Gauss sum. Since $\\chi$ is primitive, its modulus $\\mathfrak m$ is determined by $\\chi$ and is suppressed in the notation. Then $$\\label{eq:poisson}\n \\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_k\\chi(k)\\mathbf 1_{(k,\\mathfrak r)=1}\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal{H})\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\gamma(\\chi)}{\\sqrt{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}}\n   \\sum_{d\\mid\\operatorname{rad}\\mathfrak r}\\frac{\\mu(d)\\chi(d)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)}\n   \\sum_h\\overline{\\chi(h)}\n        \\widehat\\Phi\\!\\Bigl(\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(h)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}\\Bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\widehat\\Phi$ is defined by $$\\widehat\\Phi(|w|^2)=\\frac2{\\sqrt3}\\int_{\\mathbb C}\n       \\Phi(|z|^2)e(-zw)\\,dx\\,dy,\\qquad z=x+\\mathrm i y.$$ The measure is normalized so that $\\mathcal O$ has covolume one. Here $k,h\\in\\mathcal O$, and $h$ is the Fourier frequency. We have $$\\chi\\text{ nonprincipal}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\overline{\\chi(0)}=0.$$ For the principal primitive character ($\\chi=\\mathbf1$, $\\mathfrak m=1$), the zero-frequency contribution is $$\\mathcal H\\widehat\\Phi(0)\n   \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid \\mathfrak r}\\Bigl(1-\\frac1{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak p)}\\Bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-003.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-poisson-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\vartheta\\le1/10$ and put $H=D^{1+\\vartheta}$. For each fixed $C\\ge1$ and all real $B,F\\ge1$, consider the ranges $$\\label{eq:initial-scales}\n X=\\frac D{BF},\\qquad 0<\\mathcal{H}\\le\\frac{CD^2}{HB^2},\\qquad\n XF=\\frac DB.$$ Suppose that for every $\\varepsilon>0$ there exists an integer $J=J(\\vartheta,\\varepsilon)\\ge1$ such that, for every compact interval $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$ and every smooth $W$ supported in $I$, $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-target}\n \\mathcal E(\\mathcal H,X,F;\\xi,W)\n \\ll_{\\nu,S,I,C,\\vartheta,\\varepsilon}\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2D^\\varepsilon XF,$$ where $$\\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}=\\max_{0\\le j\\le J}\\sup_{x\\in I}|W^{(j)}(x)|.$$ For functions of several variables, the norm uses all partial derivatives of total order at most $J$. Then the original mean-square estimate in Proposition <a href=\"#thm:ms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:ms\">3</a> holds.",
        "source_id": "PM-003.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-r",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\varepsilon>0$, $C_0\\ge1$, and a character $\\xi$ of the fixed ray class group.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-realization",
        "exact_quote": "With these definitions, $$\\label{eq:T-theta}\n T(X;k,f)=\\frac1{3^{5/2}\\sqrt X}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n,b\\in\\mathcal O\\\\n,b\\equiv1\\ (3)\\\\n\\ {\\rm squarefree}\\\\(nb,S)=1}}\n c_\\theta(nb^3)\\phi_k(nb^3)\\overline{\\alpha(nb^3)}\n W\\!\\Bigl(\\frac{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(nb^3)}X\\Bigr).$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:theta-twist-translates}\n \\Theta_k(z,v)=\\sum_{h\\in\\mathcal O/(q)}\\widehat\\phi_k(h)\n       \\overline{\\theta(z+\\lambda^2h/q,v)}.$$",

      },

          "source_id": "PM-003.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For each $d\\in\\{d_0,d_+,d_-\\}$, the coefficient $d(\\ell)$ vanishes unless $\\ell$ can be written as $$\\label{eq:theta-support}\n \\ell=u\\lambda^mnb^3,$$ where $u\\in\\mathcal O^\\times$, $m\\in\\mathbb Z$ with $m\\ge-4$, and $n,b\\in\\mathcal O$ are primary with $n$ squarefree. $$\\label{eq:theta-coefficient-bound}\n |d(\\ell)|=|d(u\\lambda^mnb^3)|\\le27\\cdot 3^{m/6}|b|.$$*\n\n\n*For $A>0$, integers $j\\ge0$, and $V_*$ supported in a fixed compact interval $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$, there is $J=J(A,j)$ such that $$\\label{eq:theta-weight-decay}\n |(x\\partial_x)^jV_*^\\sharp(x)|\n \\ll_{A,j,I}\\|V_*\\|_{C^J(I)}\\min(x^{1/4},x^{-A}),\\qquad x>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-003.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-theta-bounds",

        "exact_quote": "Fix a box $\\mathcal I=I_1\\times\\cdots\\times I_d$, where each $I_j$ is a compact interval in $(0,\\infty)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-smooth-mean-square",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two families of finite sums $$S_{j,r}(U)=\\sum_n a_{j,r}(n)U(x_{j,r,n}),\\qquad\n j=1,2,\\quad x_{j,r,n}>0,$$ with a finite set of rows $r$ and nonnegative weights $w_r$.         "source_id": "PM-003.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Eisenstein integer ring** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Write $\mathcal O=\mathcal O_K=\mathbb Z[\omega]$, where $\omega=e^{2\pi\mathrm i/3}$.
- **Element norm** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For $a\in K$, write $\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(a)=a\overline a=\|a\|^2$ for its norm.
- **Integral ideal norm** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a nonzero integral ideal $\mathfrak a\subseteq\mathcal O$, write $\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\mathfrak a)=\#(\mathcal O/\mathfrak a)$.
- **Primary element** ([source](#eq:intro-family)): We call an element of $\mathcal O$ *primary* if it is congruent to $1$ modulo $3$.
- **Dual mean square** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): The parameters $\mathcal H$, $X$, and $F$ are the norm scales of $k$, $n$, and $f$, respectively. Here $k$ ranges over elements of $\mathcal O$, and a star restricts a sum to squarefree ideals prime to $S$, represented by their primary generators. For a smooth compactly supported weight $W$ on $(0,\infty)$, define the *dual mean square* by $$\label{eq:energy} \begin{aligned} &\mathcal E(\mathcal{H},X,F;\xi,W)\\ &\qquad=\frac1{XF} \sum_{\substack{F\le\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(f)<2F\\(f,S)=1}}^*\sum_{0<\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(k)\le \mathcal{H}} \Bigl\|\sum_{(n,S)=1}^*a_\xi(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4 W(\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(n)/X)\Bigr\|^2. \end{aligned}$$
- **Completed theta sum** ([source](#eq:T)): Define $$\label{eq:T} T(X;\Psi)= \sum_{(n,S)=1}^*\sum_{\substack{b\in\mathcal O\\b\equiv1\ (3)\\(b,S)=1}} \frac{\overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n)\Psi(n) \overline{\alpha(b)}^{\,3}\Psi(b)^3} {\sqrt{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(n)}\,\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(b)}\,V_*(\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(n)\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(b)^3/X),$$ where $V_*(y)=\sqrt y\,W(y)$.
- **Row and auxiliary twist** ([source](#eq:completed-twist)): For a nonzero row $k\in\mathcal O$ and a squarefree primary $f$ prime to $S$, set $$\label{eq:completed-twist} \Psi_k(n):=\xi(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4, \qquad T(X;k,f):=T(X;\Psi_k).$$
- **Local reflected character factor** ([source](#eq:theta-local-factors)): For a primary prime $p\notin S$, $j\in\{0,\ldots,5\}$, and $x\in\mathcal O$, define the local factor $$\label{eq:theta-local-factors} B_{p,j}(x)= \begin{cases} \chi_p(x)^{-j-2},&j\ne0,4,\\ \mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(p)^{-1/2}(-1+\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(p)\mathbf 1_{p\mid x}),&j=4,\\ \mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(p)^{-1/2}\chi_p(x)^{-2},&j=0. \end{cases}$$
- **Smoothed energy** ([source](#eq:smoothed-energy)): For fixed $\mathcal H,L,F,\xi$, put $$\label{eq:smoothed-energy} \mathcal A(W):=\frac1{LF} \sum_{\substack{F\le N(f)<2F\\(f,S)=1}}^* \sum_{k\in\mathcal O}\Phi(N(k)/\mathcal H) \Bigl\|\sum_{(n,S)=1}^* a_\xi(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4W(N(n)/L)\Bigr\|^2.$$
- **Auxiliary nonnegative transfer form** ([source](#eq:first-transfer-forms)): The nonnegative form required in the second Poisson calculation is $$\label{eq:first-transfer-forms} \mathcal Q_{\xi_1}(U)= \sum_{\substack{C,t\text{ squarefree}\\(Ct,S)=1, (C,t)=1\\ N(C)N(t)\le2vL}} \sum_{d\mid C}w_{C,d} \sum_y\Phi(N(y)/Y_{C,d})\|P_{C,d,t}(y;U)\|^2.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-zero-free-boundary | \operatorname{Re}s | > | $11/12$ | Uniform in the character conductor and height. |
| q-row-expansion-ceiling | \vartheta | ≤ | $1/10$ | The row expansion parameter is fixed before the size parameter varies. |
| q-original-row-scale | H | = | $D^{1+\vartheta}$ | The row scale is determined by the original size and fixed expansion parameter. |
| q-cube-cutoff | H_c^3 | = | $\min\!\Bigl(X,\frac{X^2}{\mathcal H^2}\Bigr)$ | Determined by the current row and column scales. |
| q-reduced-column-scale | L_b | = | $\frac{X}{\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(b)^3}$ | The cube index varies over primary elements prime to the excluded set. |
| q-child-row-ceiling | \mathcal H' | ≤ | $\frac{\mathcal HL}{\Sigma F}$ | Every retained child energy satisfies the stated row ceiling. |
| q-child-relative-row-ceiling | \frac{\mathcal H'}{\Sigma'} | ≤ | $\frac{\mathcal H}{\Sigma}$ | The target scale of a child is its column and auxiliary product. |
| q-child-target-ceiling | \Sigma' | ≤ | $L$ | The child target product is bounded by the current column scale. |
| q-cusp-coefficient-ceiling | \|d(\ell)\| | ≤ | $27\cdot 3^{m/6}\|b\|$ | The bound applies to every stated cusp coefficient sequence. |
| q-reflection-scalar-ceiling | \|C\| | ≤ | $1/81$ | Fixed ray data and an active set are held fixed within each group. |
| q-smooth-separation-regularity | q | > | $J+d$ | The derivative order is even and exceeds the moment order plus dimension. |
| q-kernel-derivative-order | q | = | $2m+4$ | The derivative order ensures convergence of the bilinear Mellin integral. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-ray-arithmetic** [claim](#stmt-lem-arithmetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-transfer-3): Gauss–Jacobi identities, quadratic Gauss-sum evaluation, and reciprocity. -> Reciprocity factors and mutually compatible conversions between Möbius and cubic Gauss-sum coefficients.
- **step-excluded-poisson** [claim](#stmt-lem-poisson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-poisson): Inclusion–exclusion followed by lattice Poisson summation. -> An explicit dual frequency sum and its principal zero-frequency term.
- **step-remove-exclusions** [claim](#stmt-lem-remove-exclusions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-remove-exclusions): Inclusion–exclusion, coefficient multiplicativity, and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> Unexcluded energies with unchanged row range and target product.
- **step-separate-smooth-weights** [claim](#stmt-lem-smooth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-smooth): Fourier inversion in logarithmic coordinates and integration by parts. -> Separated multiplicative phases with weighted integrable coefficients.
- **step-recombine-integrals** [claim](#stmt-lem-recombine) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-recombine): Weighted Cauchy–Schwarz followed by summation over rows. -> A mean-square bound for the recombined integral.
- **step-row-dependent-kernels** [claim](#stmt-lem-smooth-mean-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-smooth-mean-square): Mellin separation and Cauchy–Schwarz with a common coefficient majorant. -> A bilinear kernel estimate controlled by the geometric mean of the input bounds.
- **step-theta-realization** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-theta-realization): Patterson’s coefficient formula and finite Fourier inversion. -> A realization by theta coefficients and a finite translate expansion.
- **step-reflect-theta** [claim](#stmt-lem-reflection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-transfer-4): Finite local Fourier expansion, cusp automorphy, and Mellin contour shifting. -> Dual cusp sums with explicit local factors and the transformed weight.
- **step-uniform-cusp-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-reflection-uniformity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-transfer-4): Track reduced denominators and cusp choices within fixed ray classes. -> Coefficient sequences and additive characters independent of the row within each fixed ray class.
- **step-bound-cusp-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-transfer-4): Explicit cusp coefficient formulas and contour shifts for the weight transform. -> Coefficient support and magnitude bounds together with transformed-weight decay.
- **step-quadratic-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadratic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic): Apply the number-field quadratic large sieve after fixing supplementary characters and ray classes. -> A quadratic mean-square estimate with the sum of row and column lengths.
- **step-squarefree-completed** [claim](#stmt-lem-squarefree-completed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-squarefree-completed): Isolate shared prime factors, reflect theta, reindex exceptional local factors, and apply the quadratic sieve. -> The completed estimate for squarefree rows, including rows meeting the excluded set.
- **step-all-completed-rows** [claim](#stmt-prop-r) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-squarefree-completed-2): Factor each row into a unit, a squarefree part, and a square, then sum the convergent reciprocal-norm series. -> The completed mean-square estimate for arbitrary nonzero rows.
- **step-cube-inversion** [claim](#stmt-lem-cube-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cube-reduction): Möbius inversion, a short-divisor cutoff, and weighted Cauchy–Schwarz. -> A controlled short contribution and remaining energies at shorter column scales.
- **step-first-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-first-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-first-transfer): Expand the square, apply Poisson summation, remove column coprimality, and enlarge the nonnegative row family. -> Control of the smoothed energy by the auxiliary nonnegative form.
- **step-second-poisson-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-arithmetic-poisson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arithmetic-poisson): Extract common factors, apply Poisson summation, and remove the remaining mutual column coprimality. -> An exact second Poisson identity with cubic coefficients and a bounded zero frequency.
- **step-second-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-second-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-transfer): Regroup signed preimages before taking absolute values, partition scales, remove exclusions, and separate kernels. -> An auxiliary-form bound with contracted child row scales.
- **step-transfer-proposition** [claim](#stmt-prop-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-transfer-2): Compose the transfer lemmas and allocate the small power losses. -> The transfer bound and all child scale inequalities.
- **step-canonical-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-canonical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transfer): Induct on the row scale using the positive gap and the contraction supplied by cube reduction and transfer. -> The canonical dual mean-square estimate with finite derivative loss.
- **step-initial-poisson-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-poisson-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-poisson-reduction): Expand the original square, extract common factors, apply Poisson summation, and identify the normalized dual family. -> The original mean-square estimate from its dual counterpart.
- **step-complete-original-mean-square** [claim](#stmt-thm-ms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transfer-2): Check the positive gap in the initial scales and handle bounded or empty ranges by counting. -> The uniform original Möbius mean-square estimate.
- **step-zero-free-conclusion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-ms): Extract prime sixth-power rows, obtain a Möbius power saving, and use Mellin continuation and quadratic base change. -> The asserted Hecke and Dirichlet zero-free half-plane.
- **step-prime-progressions** [claim](#stmt-cor-primes-ap) / [proof](#sec:intro): The cited explicit-formula argument. -> The uniform effective prime-counting discrepancy bound.

This means that $\\mathcal R(a,b)=\\mathcal R(b,a)$ and $\\mathcal R$ is multiplicative in each argument separately: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal R(aa',b)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a',b),\\\\\n \\mathcal R(a,bb')&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a,b'),\n \\end{aligned}$$ for all classes $a,a',b,b'$ in the ray class group. These functions have the following properties. Let $a,b,n$ be primary elements prime to $S$, with $(a,b)=1$ and $n$ squarefree. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi_b(a)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\chi_a(b),&\n G(ab)&=G(a)G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b),                                      \\label{eq:recip}\\\\\n \\gamma_2(n)^3&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_2(n)&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n)G(n),                 \\label{eq:gj}\\\\\n G(n)&=\\overline{\\chi_n(4)}\\gamma_3(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)&=\\chi_n(-1).                     \\label{eq:g}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n)\\gamma_1(n)&=\\mu(n)G(n),\\label{eq:convert1}\\\\\n \\mu(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)\n &=\\chi_n(-1)G(n)^{-1}\\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n),\\label{eq:convert2}\\\\\n a_\\xi(ab)&=a_\\xi(a)a_\\xi(b)\\chi_b(a)^4,                     \\label{eq:crt-a}\\\\\n \\chi_a(-1)\\overline{G(a)}G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b)&=G(ba^{-1}).            \\label{eq:quotient}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In <a href=\"#eq:crt-a\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:crt-a\">[eq:crt-a]</a>, $a,b$ are also squarefree.           "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H>0$, let $\\mathfrak r$ be a nonzero ideal, and let $\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal H)$ be a smooth radial Schwartz weight on the row lattice. Let $\\chi$ be a primitive multiplicative character of $(\\mathcal O/\\mathfrak m)^\\times$, viewed as a function on $\\mathcal O$ by reduction modulo $\\mathfrak m$ and extension by zero on nonunits. Write $$\\gamma(\\chi)=\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)^{-1/2}\n\\sum_{x\\bmod\\mathfrak m}\\chi(x)e(x/\\mathfrak m)$$ for its normalized Gauss sum. Since $\\chi$ is primitive, its modulus $\\mathfrak m$ is determined by $\\chi$ and is suppressed in the notation. Then $$\\label{eq:poisson}\n \\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_k\\chi(k)\\mathbf 1_{(k,\\mathfrak r)=1}\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal{H})\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\gamma(\\chi)}{\\sqrt{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}}\n   \\sum_{d\\mid\\operatorname{rad}\\mathfrak r}\\frac{\\mu(d)\\chi(d)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)}\n   \\sum_h\\overline{\\chi(h)}\n        \\widehat\\Phi\\!\\Bigl(\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(h)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}\\Bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\widehat\\Phi$ is defined by $$\\widehat\\Phi(|w|^2)=\\frac2{\\sqrt3}\\int_{\\mathbb C}\n       \\Phi(|z|^2)e(-zw)\\,dx\\,dy,\\qquad z=x+\\mathrm i y.$$ The measure is normalized so that $\\mathcal O$ has covolume one. Here $k,h\\in\\mathcal O$, and $h$ is the Fourier frequency. We have $$\\chi\\text{ nonprincipal}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\overline{\\chi(0)}=0.$$ For the principal primitive character ($\\chi=\\mathbf1$, $\\mathfrak m=1$), the zero-frequency contribution is $$\\mathcal H\\widehat\\Phi(0)\n   \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid \\mathfrak r}\\Bigl(1-\\frac1{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak p)}\\Bigr).$$",

If $M\\ge0$ satisfies $\\mathcal Q_{\\xi_1}(U)\\le M\\|U\\|_{C^j(I_*)}^2$ for every $U\\in C_c^\\infty(I_*)$ and every $\\xi_1$, then $$\\mathcal A(W)\\ll D^{\\varepsilon_0}(\\Sigma+M)\n                    \\|W\\|_{C^{2j+4}(I)}^2.$$",

          "exact_quote": "With the notation and index ranges above, $$\\label{eq:arithmetic-poisson-identity}\n \\mathcal M=Z+\n \\sum_{g,e,w,h,\\xi'}\\frac{Y\\mu(e)\\mu(w)c_{\\xi'}N(g)}{N(e)\\ell} \\sum_{(n_1n_2,S)=1}^*a^\\sharp(n_1)\\overline{a^\\sharp(n_2)}\n       \\mathscr K\\!\\Bigl(\\frac{N(n_1)}{X'},\\frac{N(n_2)}{X'}\\Bigr).$$ The zero-frequency term satisfies $$|Z|\\ll_{I_*,\\Phi}Y\\ell\\|U\\|_\\infty^2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"
        }
      ],

      "inputs": [

      "method": "Extract common factors, apply Poisson summation, and remove the remaining mutual column coprimality.",

Then, for every $\\varepsilon_0>0$, $$\\mathcal Q_{\\xi_1}(U)\\ll D^{\\varepsilon_0}\\Sigma(1+S_m)\n       \\|U\\|_{C^{2m+4}(I_*)}^2\n \\qquad(U\\in C_c^\\infty(I_*)).$$ The test functions in $S_m$ are supported in $I'=[u/16,4v]$ and have $C^m(I')$ norm at most one; an empty supremum is zero.",

          "exact_quote": "Assume $1\\le\\mathcal H,L,F,\\Sigma\\le D^{C_0}$ and $\\max\\{\\mathcal H,LF\\}\\le\\Sigma$, for fixed $C_0\\ge1$. For every integer $m\\ge0$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, $$\\label{eq:transfer-summary}\n \\mathcal A(W)\\ll D^\\varepsilon\\Sigma\\|W\\|_{C^{4m+12}(I)}^2\n \\Bigl(1+\\sup\\frac{\\mathcal E(\\mathcal H',X',F';\\xi',U)}{\\Sigma'}\\Bigr)$$ where the supremum is over the family <a href=\"#eq:energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:energy\">[eq:energy]</a>, with $\\Sigma'=X'F'$, $\\mathcal H',X',F'\\ge1$, and $$\\label{eq:transfer-scales}\n \\mathcal H'\\le\\frac{\\mathcal HL}{\\Sigma F},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\mathcal H'}{\\Sigma'}\\le\n \\frac{\\mathcal H}{\\Sigma},\\qquad\n \\Sigma'\\le L.$$ For $I=[u,v]$, the tests satisfy $U\\in C_c^\\infty(I')$, $I'=[u/16,4v]$, and $\\|U\\|_{C^m(I')}\\le1$; an empty supremum is zero. The estimate holds for every compact $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$ and $W\\in C_c^\\infty(I)$, with implied constant depending on $m,C_0,\\varepsilon,I,\\nu,S$ and the fixed ray class group.",

Suppose that $$\\label{eq:positive-gap}\n \\mathcal H,X,F\\ge1,\\qquad\n \\Sigma=XF\\le D^{C_0},\\qquad\n \\mathcal H\\le\\Sigma D^{-\\kappa}.$$ For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is an integer $J=J(\\kappa,C_0,\\varepsilon)\\ge1$ such that $$\\label{eq:canonical-bound}\n \\mathcal E(\\mathcal H,X,F;\\xi,W)\n \\ll_{I,\\nu,S,\\kappa,C_0,\\varepsilon}\n \\|W\\|_{C^J(I)}^2D^\\varepsilon\\Sigma$$ for every compact interval $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$ and smooth $W$ supported in $I$.",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"

Then the original mean-square estimate in Proposition <a href=\"#thm:ms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:ms\">3</a> holds.",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"
        }

    },

          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $0<\\vartheta\\le1/10$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, there exists an integer $k=k(\\vartheta,\\varepsilon)\\ge1$ such that, for every compact interval $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$, all smooth $W$ supported in $I$, and $D\\ge2$, $$\\label{eq:ms}\n \\sum_{0<\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(u)\\le D^{1+\\vartheta}}|A_u(D)|^2\n       \\ll_{\\nu,S,I,\\vartheta,\\varepsilon}\n       \\Bigl(\\max_{0\\le j\\le k}\\|W^{(j)}\\|_\\infty\\Bigr)^2\n       D^{2+\\vartheta+\\varepsilon}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"
        }

      "output": "The uniform original Möbius mean-square estimate.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-transfer-2"

          "source_id": "PM-003.2"

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi(x;q,a)$ count the primes $p\\le x$ with $p\\equiv a\\pmod q$. Write $\\varphi$ for Euler’s totient function. For $x\\ge2$, $$\\sup_{\\substack{1\\le q\\le x\\\\(a,q)=1}}\n \\biggl|\\pi(x;q,a)-\\frac1{\\varphi(q)}\n                  \\int_2^x\\frac{\\,dt}{\\log t}\\biggr|\n \\ll x^{11/12}\\log x,$$ where the implied constant is absolute and effective.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-zero-free-family**: The asserted zero-free half-plane covers finite-order Hecke functions over the specified Eisenstein field and, by the stated deduction, Dirichlet functions including the Riemann zeta function.
- **invariant-transfer-admissibility**: The transfer estimate controls the smoothed energy by admissible child mean squares while preserving the upper bound on the row-to-target ratio and reducing the available target scale.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-general-number-fields**: Apply the main zero-free assertion to Hecke functions over an arbitrary number field. -> The asserted family is restricted to the specified field; the Dirichlet conclusion does not supply an extension to arbitrary fields.
- **anti-discard-completion**: Obtain a bound for the squarefree-index sum by deleting the extra cube-index terms from the completed sum. -> Cancellation between cube-index contributions prevents such deletion from preserving a bound.
- **anti-unrestricted-prime-progressions**: Use the prime-counting corollary for arbitrary residue classes and unrestricted moduli. -> The displayed supremum only controls the stated coprime residue classes and modulus range.
- **anti-transfer-without-gap**: Conclude that recursive descent proves the canonical estimate without checking the positive gap. -> The canonical proof uses the gap to contract child row scales; the transfer estimate alone does not establish that contraction in an unrestricted regime.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The source states: "Every finite-order Hecke $L$-function over $K$ has no zeros in the half-plane $\operatorname{Re}s>11/12$." This universally quantified analytic assertion concerns an unbounded family and a continuous half-plane. Its proof uses analytic continuation, theta transformations, and uniform asymptotic mean-square estimates. Finite test vectors cannot decide that assertion or certify the uniform estimates. The introductory computational consequences do not provide a finite implementation contract for the analytic theorem.

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-poisson",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H>0$, let $\\mathfrak r$ be a nonzero ideal, and let $\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal H)$ be a smooth radial Schwartz weight on the row lattice. Let $\\chi$ be a primitive multiplicative character of $(\\mathcal O/\\mathfrak m)^\\times$, viewed as a function on $\\mathcal O$ by reduction modulo $\\mathfrak m$ and extension by zero on nonunits. Write $$\\gamma(\\chi)=\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)^{-1/2}\n\\sum_{x\\bmod\\mathfrak m}\\chi(x)e(x/\\mathfrak m)$$ for its normalized Gauss sum. Since $\\chi$ is primitive, its modulus $\\mathfrak m$ is determined by $\\chi$ and is suppressed in the notation. Then $$\\label{eq:poisson}\n \\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_k\\chi(k)\\mathbf 1_{(k,\\mathfrak r)=1}\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal{H})\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\gamma(\\chi)}{\\sqrt{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}}\n   \\sum_{d\\mid\\operatorname{rad}\\mathfrak r}\\frac{\\mu(d)\\chi(d)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)}\n   \\sum_h\\overline{\\chi(h)}\n        \\widehat\\Phi\\!\\Bigl(\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(h)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}\\Bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\widehat\\Phi$ is defined by $$\\widehat\\Phi(|w|^2)=\\frac2{\\sqrt3}\\int_{\\mathbb C}\n       \\Phi(|z|^2)e(-zw)\\,dx\\,dy,\\qquad z=x+\\mathrm i y.$$ The measure is normalized so that $\\mathcal O$ has covolume one. Here $k,h\\in\\mathcal O$, and $h$ is the Fourier frequency. We have $$\\chi\\text{ nonprincipal}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\overline{\\chi(0)}=0.$$ For the principal primitive character ($\\chi=\\mathbf1$, $\\mathfrak m=1$), the zero-frequency contribution is $$\\mathcal H\\widehat\\Phi(0)\n   \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid \\mathfrak r}\\Bigl(1-\\frac1{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak p)}\\Bigr).$$",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-poisson"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-hecke-field-and-character-scope** (formal_scope): The Hecke nonvanishing assertion concerns finite-order characters over the specified Eisenstein field; the Dirichlet deduction does not extend its Hecke scope to arbitrary number fields. Blocked use: ['Apply the Hecke conclusion to arbitrary number fields or characters outside the stated finite-order family.']
- **limit-strict-half-plane** (edge_case): The zero-free assertion is for a strict half-plane. It does not settle zeros on its boundary or establish the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Include the boundary in the asserted zero-free region.', 'Claim that all critical-strip zeros lie on the critical line.']
- **limit-prime-progression-regime** (hypothesis): The prime-counting discrepancy estimate requires the displayed size, modulus, and coprimality conditions, and its effective implied constant is not numerically specified in the statement. Blocked use: ['Apply the corollary to unrestricted moduli or non-coprime residue classes.', 'Treat the displayed asymptotic expression as a numerical error certificate without an explicit implied constant.']
- **limit-original-mean-square-test-family** (hypothesis): The original mean-square estimate fixes the row expansion parameter and exponent loss before choosing a derivative order. Its uniform weight control is restricted to smooth weights supported in a fixed compact interval. Blocked use: ['Substitute a sharp cutoff without a separate smoothing argument.', 'Vary the support or row expansion parameter while claiming unchanged implied constants or derivative requirements.']
- **limit-arithmetic-squarefreeness-and-exclusions** (hypothesis): The arithmetic coefficient identities retain their stated primary, coprimality, squarefreeness, and excluded-prime conditions. The extension of the ray class identity for the fixed factor does not extend every Gauss-sum identity to arbitrary elements. Blocked use: ['Use the squarefree coefficient conversion for unrestricted prime powers.', 'Drop the coprimality or excluded-prime assumptions in the coefficient multiplication identity.']
- **limit-poisson-primitivity-and-zero-frequency** (hypothesis): The Poisson formula assumes a primitive character and a smooth radial Schwartz weight. Its nonprincipal zero-frequency vanishing must not replace the separate principal contribution. Blocked use: ['Use the normalized primitive Gauss-sum formula for an imprimitive character without reduction.', 'Delete the principal zero-frequency term.', 'Apply the formula to an arbitrary nonsmooth row cutoff without justification.']
- **limit-exclusion-removal-scale-shifts** (hypothesis): Removing column exclusions introduces divisor sums and changes the column and auxiliary scales together. A downstream estimate must remain valid at all resulting scales. Blocked use: ['Remove an exclusion while retaining only the original column and auxiliary ranges.', 'Ignore the divisor factor or the shifted energies.']
- **limit-initial-reduction-uniformity** (hypothesis): The initial reduction requires a dual estimate uniform over the specified initial scales and all smooth tests in each compact support interval, with the stated derivative norm. Blocked use: ['Deduce the original mean square from a dual estimate for a single test function or a single initial scale.', 'Suppress the dependence on the derivative norm during weight separation.']
- **limit-canonical-positive-gap** (hypothesis): The canonical bound requires a fixed positive power gap between the row scale and the target product, as well as the stated lower and upper scale bounds. Blocked use: ['Apply the canonical estimate when the row scale reaches the target product without a positive gap.', 'Claim uniformity as the fixed gap parameter degenerates.']
- **limit-theta-completion-cancellation** (hypothesis): The squarefree-index part cannot be bounded merely by deleting the additional cube-index terms from a completed sum, because those contributions can cancel. Blocked use: ['Transfer the completed mean-square bound directly to the squarefree-index sum by term deletion.']
- **limit-completed-bound-auxiliary-scale** (hypothesis): The completed estimate retains the auxiliary norm in its bound and assumes a squarefree primary auxiliary index prime to the excluded set. General auxiliary twists require the separate squarefree-row result and the subsequent row decomposition. Blocked use: ['Discard the auxiliary norm dependence.', 'Apply the arbitrary-row completed proposition directly to an unrestricted auxiliary twist.']
- **limit-cube-inversion-remainder** (hypothesis): Cube inversion separates a controlled contribution from a supremum over shorter column scales. The remainder is absent only under the stated empty-supremum condition; otherwise it requires the transfer argument. Blocked use: ['Discard the long-divisor remainder whenever cube inversion is performed.', 'Replace an empty supremum convention by a claim that all remainder ranges are empty.']
- **limit-transfer-admissible-child-tests** (hypothesis): The transfer estimate requires its target scale to dominate the row scale and the column-auxiliary product. Its child energies have restricted scales, enlarged support, and normalized derivative bounds. Blocked use: ['Choose child energies outside the stated scale inequalities.', 'Apply an induction hypothesis on the original support interval without checking the enlarged child support.', 'Ignore the derivative cost when composing transfers.']
- **limit-reflection-uniformity-before-averaging** (hypothesis): The reflected cusp coefficient sequences and additive characters are controlled within fixed ray classes of the varying squarefree row. Their dependence must be fixed before applying a common-coefficient quadratic mean-square estimate. Blocked use: ['Treat reflected coefficient sequences as globally independent of the varying row without the ray class partition.']
- **limit-signed-transfer-regrouping** (hypothesis): The second transfer proof regroups signed preimages before taking absolute values. Its child column lower bound uses the surviving divisibility restriction, so replacing the signed regrouping by an unrestricted positive enlargement loses a stated justification. Blocked use: ['Take absolute values before the signed preimages are recombined.', 'Use the child column lower bound after dropping the surviving divisibility restriction.']
- **limit-smooth-separation-regularity** (hypothesis): Smooth separation assumes compact support in a fixed box bounded away from zero and enough derivatives for the required coefficient moment. It is an integral representation rather than a finite quadrature guarantee. Blocked use: ['Use the coefficient moment estimate at an insufficient derivative order.', 'Convert the representation into a certified finite numerical transform without truncation and approximation bounds.']
- **limit-row-kernel-common-test-control** (hypothesis): The row-dependent kernel estimate requires common-test-function mean-square bounds for every admissible smooth test, nonnegative row weights, and uniform kernel derivative control. Blocked use: ['Infer the kernel estimate from agreement on finitely many test functions.', 'Use independently varying row kernels without their uniform derivative bound.']
- **limit-recombination-integrable-majorant** (hypothesis): Recombination requires a common integrable majorant for the coefficient functions and finiteness of the right-hand side. Blocked use: ['Interchange parameter integration and row summation without the stated integrability conditions.', 'Use cancellation in the coefficient functions as a substitute for the common absolute majorant.']
- **limit-finite-testing-of-analytic-assertion** (nonconstructive): The universal analytic nonvanishing assertion and its uniform asymptotic estimates are not decided by checking finitely many characters, heights, or norm scales. Blocked use: ['Present finite numerical agreement as a certificate of the main theorem.', 'Treat the finite analytic descent as an executable certificate for the entire zero-free half-plane.']
- **limit-computational-consequence-contract** (formal_scope): The introduction states deterministic polynomial-time arithmetic consequences, but does not give an explicit nonresidue search cutoff or a complete implementation contract. The modular square-root consequence is stated for an odd prime modulus. Blocked use: ['Extend the modular square-root consequence to arbitrary composite moduli.', 'Infer a practical search budget or superiority over randomized methods from the qualitative polynomial-time consequence.']

        }

      "id": "limit-original-mean-square-test-family",
      "kind": "hypothesis",

      "blocked_use": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arithmetic",
          "exact_quote": "On a fixed ray class group, there are a function $G$ with values in $\\{z\\in\\mathbb C:|z|=1\\}$ and a symmetric $\\{\\pm1\\}$-valued bicharacter $\\mathcal R$. This means that $\\mathcal R(a,b)=\\mathcal R(b,a)$ and $\\mathcal R$ is multiplicative in each argument separately: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal R(aa',b)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a',b),\\\\\n \\mathcal R(a,bb')&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\mathcal R(a,b'),\n \\end{aligned}$$ for all classes $a,a',b,b'$ in the ray class group. These functions have the following properties. Let $a,b,n$ be primary elements prime to $S$, with $(a,b)=1$ and $n$ squarefree. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi_b(a)&=\\mathcal R(a,b)\\chi_a(b),&\n G(ab)&=G(a)G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b),                                      \\label{eq:recip}\\\\\n \\gamma_2(n)^3&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_2(n)&=\\mu(n)\\alpha(n)G(n),                 \\label{eq:gj}\\\\\n G(n)&=\\overline{\\chi_n(4)}\\gamma_3(n),&\n \\gamma_1(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)&=\\chi_n(-1).                     \\label{eq:g}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n)\\gamma_1(n)&=\\mu(n)G(n),\\label{eq:convert1}\\\\\n \\mu(n)\\gamma_{-1}(n)\n &=\\chi_n(-1)G(n)^{-1}\\overline{\\alpha(n)}\\gamma_2(n),\\label{eq:convert2}\\\\\n a_\\xi(ab)&=a_\\xi(a)a_\\xi(b)\\chi_b(a)^4,                     \\label{eq:crt-a}\\\\\n \\chi_a(-1)\\overline{G(a)}G(b)\\mathcal R(a,b)&=G(ba^{-1}).            \\label{eq:quotient}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In <a href=\"#eq:crt-a\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:crt-a\">[eq:crt-a]</a>, $a,b$ are also squarefree. The identity for $G(ab)$ in <a href=\"#eq:recip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:recip\">[eq:recip]</a> extends to all classes of the fixed ray class group; the quotient in <a href=\"#eq:quotient\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quotient\">[eq:quotient]</a> is taken in that group.",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-arithmetic-squarefreeness-and-exclusions",

      "blocked_use": [
        "Use the normalized primitive Gauss-sum formula for an imprimitive character without reduction.",
        "Delete the principal zero-frequency term.",
        "Apply the formula to an arbitrary nonsmooth row cutoff without justification."

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-poisson",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H>0$, let $\\mathfrak r$ be a nonzero ideal, and let $\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal H)$ be a smooth radial Schwartz weight on the row lattice. Let $\\chi$ be a primitive multiplicative character of $(\\mathcal O/\\mathfrak m)^\\times$, viewed as a function on $\\mathcal O$ by reduction modulo $\\mathfrak m$ and extension by zero on nonunits. Write $$\\gamma(\\chi)=\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)^{-1/2}\n\\sum_{x\\bmod\\mathfrak m}\\chi(x)e(x/\\mathfrak m)$$ for its normalized Gauss sum. Since $\\chi$ is primitive, its modulus $\\mathfrak m$ is determined by $\\chi$ and is suppressed in the notation. Then $$\\label{eq:poisson}\n \\begin{aligned}\n &\\sum_k\\chi(k)\\mathbf 1_{(k,\\mathfrak r)=1}\\Phi(\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(k)/\\mathcal{H})\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\gamma(\\chi)}{\\sqrt{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}}\n   \\sum_{d\\mid\\operatorname{rad}\\mathfrak r}\\frac{\\mu(d)\\chi(d)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)}\n   \\sum_h\\overline{\\chi(h)}\n        \\widehat\\Phi\\!\\Bigl(\\frac{\\mathcal{H}\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(h)}{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(d)\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak m)}\\Bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\widehat\\Phi$ is defined by $$\\widehat\\Phi(|w|^2)=\\frac2{\\sqrt3}\\int_{\\mathbb C}\n       \\Phi(|z|^2)e(-zw)\\,dx\\,dy,\\qquad z=x+\\mathrm i y.$$ The measure is normalized so that $\\mathcal O$ has covolume one. Here $k,h\\in\\mathcal O$, and $h$ is the Fourier frequency. We have $$\\chi\\text{ nonprincipal}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\overline{\\chi(0)}=0.$$ For the principal primitive character ($\\chi=\\mathbf1$, $\\mathfrak m=1$), the zero-frequency contribution is $$\\mathcal H\\widehat\\Phi(0)\n   \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid \\mathfrak r}\\Bigl(1-\\frac1{\\mathrm N_{K/\\mathbb Q}(\\mathfrak p)}\\Bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-003.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We establish the quasi-Riemann hypothesis by proving that every Dirichlet <i>L</i>-function, including Riemann's zeta function, has no zeros in the half-plane $`\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\nolimits s\gt 11/12`$. More generally, we prove the same zero-free half-plane for every finite-order Hecke <i>L</i>-function over $`K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})`$. In particular, this rules out the existence of Landau–Siegel zeros.

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### Introduction

For a primitive Dirichlet character $\chi$ of conductor $q$, the associated *Dirichlet $L$-function* is defined for $\operatorname{Re}s>1$ by the formula $$L(s,\chi)=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\chi(n)}{n^s}, \qquad \operatorname{Re}s>1,$$ and admits a meromorphic continuation to $s \in \mathbb C$ <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[IK04](#ref-IK04), 4.6]. When $\chi$ is the trivial character, $L(s, \chi)$ recovers the Riemann zeta function $\zeta(s)$ <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Rie59](#ref-Rie59)].

The zeros of Dirichlet $L$-functions are of significant interest, such as for their role in governing the distribution of primes in arithmetic progressions. The *Generalized Riemann Hypothesis* predicts that all zeros of $L(s, \chi)$ in the critical strip $0 < \operatorname{Re}s < 1$ satisfy $\operatorname{Re}s = 1/2$. For $\zeta(s)$, the weaker assertion that there exists $\varepsilon>0$ such that $\zeta(s)$ has no zeros in $\operatorname{Re}s>1-\varepsilon$ is called a *quasi-Riemann Hypothesis* (e.g., in <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BG17](#ref-BG17), 1], <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[MS02](#ref-MS02), p. 274], and <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BR18](#ref-BR18), 2]). For Dirichlet $L$-functions, we consider the analogous assertion with a single $\varepsilon>0$ valid for every primitive character $\chi$, independently of both conductor and height.

Let $K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})$. The main result of this paper is the following.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Every finite-order Hecke $L$-function over $K$ has no zeros in the half-plane $\operatorname{Re}s>11/12$. Thus every Dirichlet $L$-function, including $\zeta(s)$, has no zeros for $\operatorname{Re}s>11/12$.*

</div>

Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">3</a> proves the theorem assuming Proposition <a href="#thm:ms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ms">3</a>, whose proof is completed in Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">5.6</a>.

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is a natural intermediate step toward the stronger zero-free region $\operatorname{Re}s>7/8$ established in <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[OpenAI26](#ref-OpenAI26), Theorem 1.1]. For simplicity, we have isolated Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and its proof here. By a standard explicit-formula argument (see <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Dav00](#ref-Dav00), Chapters 19–20]), Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives the following quantitative form of the prime number theorem in arithmetic progressions.

<a id="stmt-cor-primes-ap"></a>
<div id="cor:primes-ap" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $\pi(x;q,a)$ count the primes $p\le x$ with $p\equiv a\pmod q$. Write $\varphi$ for Euler’s totient function. For $x\ge2$, $$\sup_{\substack{1\le q\le x\\(a,q)=1}}
 \biggl|\pi(x;q,a)-\frac1{\varphi(q)}
                  \int_2^x\frac{\,dt}{\log t}\biggr|
 \ll x^{11/12}\log x,$$ where the implied constant is absolute and effective.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> has a number of additional arithmetic consequences. By the zero-free-region method originating with Rodosskiı̆ , in the form recorded in <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[MV07](#ref-MV07), Theorem 13.12], the least quadratic nonresidue modulo an odd prime $p$ is bounded by a fixed power of $\log p$, which in particular proves Vinogradov’s conjecture <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Vin85](#ref-Vin85)] that this nonresidue is $\ll_{\varepsilon}p^{\varepsilon}$ for every $\varepsilon>0$. See also Bhargava, Ivanyos, Mittal, and Saxena <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BIMS17](#ref-BIMS17), Theorem 6.7] for this consequence of a fixed zero-free half-plane. From a computational number theory perspective, this bound allows square roots modulo $p$ to be extracted deterministically in time polynomial in $\log p$ using the Tonelli–Shanks algorithm (see <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Galbraith12](#ref-Galbraith12), 2.9]). Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> also yields a deterministic polynomial-time implementation of Miller’s primality test <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Mil76](#ref-Mil76)]; note that polynomial-time primality testing was previously known unconditionally via the Agrawal–Kayal–Saxena algorithm <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AKS04](#ref-AKS04)]. For negative fundamental discriminants $D$, Littlewood’s short Euler-product argument <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Lit28](#ref-Lit28)], applied to the fixed zero-free half-plane in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, together with Dirichlet’s class-number formula (see <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Dav00](#ref-Dav00), Chapter 6]), yields the effective class-number bound $h(D)\gg\sqrt{|D|}/\log\log|D|$, with an absolute computable implied constant. The class-group computations of Elsenhans, Klüners, and Nicolae <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[EKN20](#ref-EKN20), Theorem 2], building on Weinberger <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Wei73](#ref-Wei73)], give a complete list of imaginary quadratic fields with class-group exponent dividing two when there are no Landau–Siegel zeros. Together with Grube’s characterization of idoneal numbers and his reduction to fundamental discriminants (see <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Kani11](#ref-Kani11), Theorem 6 and 2.3]), this shows that Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> confirms the completeness of Euler’s list of 65 idoneal numbers.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Prior work

<a id="eq:intro-classical-region"></a>
There is a long line of work establishing zero-free regions for Dirichlet $L$-functions. Hadamard and de la Vallée Poussin proved the Prime Number Theorem in 1896 by establishing that $\zeta(s)$ has no zeros on the line $\operatorname{Re}s=1$ <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Had96](#ref-Had96), [VP96](#ref-VP96)]. De la Vallée Poussin subsequently obtained a quantitative zero-free region to the left of this line <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[VP99](#ref-VP99)]. For nonprincipal primitive Dirichlet characters, the classical zero-free-region theorem of Grönwall and Titchmarsh <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Gro13](#ref-Gro13), [Tit30](#ref-Tit30)], in the modern form given in (see also <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Dav00](#ref-Dav00), Chapter 14]), gives an absolute, effective constant $c_0>0$ such that $$\label{eq:intro-classical-region}
 L(\sigma+\mathrm i t,\chi)\ne0
 \quad\text{if}\quad
 \sigma>1-\frac{c_0}{\log(q(|t|+3))},$$ with at most one exception for each primitive character. Any exception is a simple real zero, can occur only if $\chi$ is quadratic, and is called a *Landau–Siegel zero*. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> rules out the existence of such a zero.

There are two parameters in <a href="#eq:intro-classical-region" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-classical-region">[eq:intro-classical-region]</a>: the conductor $q$ and the height $|t|$. Even when $q$ is fixed, its width tends to zero with the height. Vinogradov and Korobov developed methods <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Vin58](#ref-Vin58), [Kor58](#ref-Kor58)] to prove that <a id="eq:intro-vk-region"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-vk-region}
 \zeta(\sigma+\mathrm i t)\ne0
 \quad\text{if}\quad
 \sigma>1-\frac{c_1}
 { (\log|t|)^{2/3}(\log\log|t|)^{1/3}},\qquad |t|\ge3,$$ for an absolute $c_1>0$ (Ford <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Ford02](#ref-Ford02)] gives an explicit value of $c_1$). The assertion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is a half-plane of fixed width, independent of both conductor and height.

As we will see in the outline, the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> draws on modern developments in character large sieves and metaplectic theta series. In particular, the connection between cubic Gauss sums and cubic theta coefficients <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Kub69](#ref-Kub69), [Pat77](#ref-Pat77)] links the argument to work on Patterson’s conjecture by Heath-Brown and Patterson <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[HBP79](#ref-HBP79)], Heath-Brown <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[HB00](#ref-HB00)], and Dunn and Radziwiłł . The recursive large-sieve arguments also build on Heath-Brown’s proof of the quadratic large sieve inequality <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[HB95](#ref-HB95)].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Organization

Section <a href="#sec:outline" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:outline">2</a> gives a high-level outline of the argument. Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">3</a> deduces the zero-free region from a mean-square estimate for twisted Möbius sums. Section <a href="#sec:initialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:initialization">4</a> reduces this estimate to a dual mean square with cubic Gauss-sum coefficients. Section <a href="#sec:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:descent">5</a> states the completed mean-square and transfer estimates, combines them into a recursive inequality, and proves the required mean-square bound. Sections <a href="#sec:reflection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reflection">6</a> and <a href="#sec:transfer-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer-proof">7</a> prove the completed estimate and the bounds for the remaining cube-divisor sums, respectively. Appendix <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">8</a> supplies the arithmetic identities and the detailed theta calculation. Appendix <a href="#app:weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:weights">9</a> records the smooth separation lemma used throughout.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Notation

Write $\mathcal O=\mathcal O_K=\mathbb Z[\omega]$, where $\omega=e^{2\pi\mathrm i/3}$. For $a\in K$, write $\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(a)=a\overline a=|a|^2$ for its norm. For a nonzero integral ideal $\mathfrak a\subseteq\mathcal O$, write $\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\mathfrak a)=\#(\mathcal O/\mathfrak a)$. We use $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal C}$ for the indicator of a condition $\mathcal C$. For a prime ideal represented by $p$ and a nonzero element or ideal $a$, write $v_p(a)$ for its exponent in the prime factorization of $a$.

We use standard asymptotic notation, writing $f=O(g)$ or $f\ll g$ when $|f|\le Cg$, where $g\ge0$ and $C>0$ is an absolute constant unless otherwise specified. Subscripts indicate possible dependence of the implied constant: for example, $f\ll_{\nu,W,\varepsilon}g$ means $|f|\le C_{\nu,W,\varepsilon}g$, where the constant may depend on $\nu,W,\varepsilon$ but is uniform in all other varying parameters. For nonnegative $f,g$, we write $f\asymp g$ when $f\ll g$ and $g\ll f$. A dyadic norm range is an interval $R\le\mathrm N_{K/\mathbb Q}(a)<2R$; a sum over dyadic scales uses $R=2^j$.

We use $D\ge2$ as an ambient size parameter; in the main argument, it is the original norm scale introduced in the outline below. Auxiliary scales may vary within ranges bounded by fixed powers of $D$. We write $$A\preccurlyeq B \quad\text{if}\quad A\ll_\varepsilon D^\varepsilon B
\qquad\text{for every }\varepsilon>0,$$ uniformly in the varying parameters over their stated ranges. Implied constants may also depend on the fixed data specified in each statement.

<a id="sec:outline"></a>
### Outline of the argument

In this section, we sketch the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, suppressing various coprimality conditions, local factors, and details of smoothing. The precise statements appear in Sections <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">3</a>–<a href="#sec:transfer-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer-proof">7</a>.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
